## Vault of Dragons

The Vault of Dragons was a hidden underground location in Waterdeep built long ago by dwarves. As of the late 15 century DR, the Vault could only be accessed using the Stone of Golorr, which revealed its location as well as the keys needed to enter.

Location
The exact location of the Vault of Dragons was unknown.Per the Waterdeep: Dragon Heist adventure, the Vault of Dragons has four potential canon locations, with a fifth location given in Idle Champions of the Forgotten Realms. It was said perhaps to be in the Castle Ward beneath the Pink Flumph theater, in the Sea Ward under either an old tower owned by Esvele Rosznar or a windmill in which the artist Kalain lived, in the City of the Dead below the Brandath family mausoleum, or in the North Ward beneath a villa.

Structure
The Vault was located underground with walls of mortared stone and doors made of solid stone slabs with stone handles and hinges. The only entrance was a  corridor ending at a massive gate of solid adamantine inscribed with Dwarvish runes that read "THE THREE KEYS. BRING THEM FORTH". This gate had no obvious handles or hinges, and opened by sliding into the wall only when three specific keys were brought near it.

Interior
Immediately inside the gates of the Vault was a tall foyer extending north with no sources of light. It featured three  columns supporting three crumbling bridges far overhead. The ground floor had with twelve iron doors embossed with images of dwarf warriors flanking the room. Of these doors, only the two in the far northeast corner of the room were real, with the other ten being immovable distractions. Behind the real doors was a chamber containing a  magical fresco of a battle between dwarves and goblins that could charm any creatures who viewed it, causing them to gaze upon it until they collapsed from exhaustion. Across from this fresco was an expertly-carved staircase leading up  to a long,  corridor known as the Hall of Moradin, from which the bridges above the foyer could be accessed via three archways along the eastern wall. This hall contained three pillars resembling upside-down warhammers, and the western wall was adorned with an aged mosaic of a dwarven blacksmith crafting dwarves out of metal and diamonds. This mosaic concealed a secret door leading to a dust-filled room containing copper urns filled with coins, gems, and other treasures.

Each of the three treacherous bridges ended in an adamantine door behind which was a small stone chamber dedicated to a different member of the Dwarven pantheon:
 The northernmost of these chambers featured a  statue of Gorm Gulthyn, the armored dwarf god of vigilance sometimes called "Old Fire Eyes". At its feet was a false trapdoor, and touching it triggered a trap of fire bursting from the statue's eyes.
 The southernmost chamber could only be accessed by a dwarf, and contained frescoes of dwarven smiths and an iron anvil. Upon the anvil was an inscription reading, "Let the hammer fall and the anvil ring", while hidden within the fresco was a stone hammer inscribed with "Let hearts be lifted and battles won". If the anvil was struck with the hammer, all those who heard the sound were gifted with enhanced vitality for a day.
 The middle chamber contained four suits of dwarven plate mail, an adamantine trapdoor in the center of the room, and a dwarven inscription reading, "A secret never before told will part Dumathoin's lips". The trapdoor opened if a secret was spoken aloud within the room, revealing a spiral staircase that descended  into the main vault.

Despite being so far underground, the main vault was illuminated by streams of sunlight from the ceiling. Ten large alcoves were located along the outer walls and twelve ornate columns supported the  vaulted ceiling, which was decorated with carvings of dwarves being blessed by their gods.

Defenses
The only entrance was sealed by the adamantine front gate, which could only be opened (from either the outside or inside) by bringing three nontraditional "keys" within  of it. The exact nature of these "keys" was unknown, but they could be anything from a special object to a type of creature to a song. The gate could only be closed by uttering a command word ("azaam").

In addition to the gate, the Vault was was magically protected to prevent any form of communication, scrying, or teleportation into it. The properties of the Stone of Golorr also contributed to the Vault's defenses, making it exceedingly difficult to not only find and access the Vault but even to remember information about it without the Stone.

History

As of the late 15 century DR, the Vault was very old and had fallen into disrepair.

During Dagult Neverember's tenure as Open Lord of Waterdeep, he embezzled half a million gold pieces from the city—known as "dragons" within Waterdeep—and concealed the sum within the main vault of the Vault of Dragons. He then recruited the gold dragon Aurinax to protect the Vault, who agreed to allow only Neverember or his agents near the treasure in exchanged for the dragonstaff of Ahghairon. Aurinax adopted the guise of an old dwarf named Barok Clanghammer, and began his vigil within the Vault. To further protect his treasure, Neverember hired a wizard to erase the memories of the Vault's location and means of access from both his own mind and the minds of his subordinates, and caused this information to become trapped within the Stone of Golorr. Neveremver then concealed the Stone in the Palace of Waterdeep and caused the wizard to disappear.

When Neverember was ousted from his position as Open Lord circa, he was cutoff from the Stone of Golorr—which came into the possession of Xanathar—and thus became unable to access his ill-gotten gold. During the, he dispatched a spy named Dalakhar to steal the Stone and to retrieve his treasure. However, by this time the public had come to learn about his embezzled money, and a hunt had begun to find his wealth involving everyone from individuals treasure hunters—such as the thief Braya—to major factions including the Cult of the Dragon, the Lords' Alliance, the Harpers, Force Grey, the Emerald Enclave, and the Order of the Gauntlet, but most notably the Zhentarim, the Xanathar's Thieves' Guild, the Cassalanter family, and Bregan D'aerthe. While Dalakhar succeeded in acquiring the Stone of Golorr, he would subsequently be killed by a fireball in Waterdeep's Trollskull Alley, and the Stone would fall into the hands of a Zhentarim agent named Urstul Floxin who took it to the Gralhund Villa. This would lead to an event that would come to be called the "Gralhund Villa Bloodbath".

Inhabitants
The Vault had been long abandoned by the time that Dagult Neverember made use of it in the 15 century DR. By 1492 DR, in addition to Aurinax, the Vault was the home of a single black pudding that laired in the Hall of Moradin.

## Valmorba

Valmorba was the undertaker and mortician of the Lower City Mortuary in the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Valmorba was a human woman of advanced age. She had gray eyes and hair bleached with time, gathered in a bun. She spoke in a kind, compassionate, and soft voice. As a gravekeeper, she did not tolerate anyone desecrating her cemetery, and her kind heart did not like killing, even if it were kobold graverobbers who were killed. Valmorba kept a diary written in an archaic and beautiful script.

Abilities
Valmorba's faith allowed her to use the shield of faith clerical spell.

History
Valmorba's mortuary was established in the  and offered coffins, funerals, ceremonies, and embalming to the residents of the Gate. By the, when the strife of serial murders committed by the Cult of Bhaal and the looming war with the Cult of the Absolute, Valmorba's mortuary was overwhelmed. The old woman struggled with procuring and having the coffins built in time. One of the children buried by Valmorba was Ayana, daughter of Fiona. The mother thanked the mortician for going above and beyond for the dead child and burying her teddy bear along with Ayana. Valmorba was known to waive fees when possible for the poor, especially parents burying their children. One of the children was a "water baby," a child who loved the sea. Valmorba prepared the child's body using weavemoss and styled her hair with chuttler's oil.

## Valraxaxath

Valraxaxath was a black dragon wizard who created the Dragons' Claw, a portal network that spanned Faerûn.

Personality
Valraxaxath believed that dragons should learn to work together in order to hoard all the wealth in the world. He was cunning, and highly skilled when it came to getting his way with others.

Abilities
Valraxaxath was a powerful wizard, and knew many spells. Through his arcane studies, he had developed the ability to create portals.

Activities
Valraxaxath made his lair in the semi-submerged ruins of a grand building in the Great Swamp of Rethild. There he kept the largest part of his hoard.

The lair also contained two portals, one that was part of the Dragons' Claw. The second portal connected his lair to a cave hidden in the Wyrmbones, where he kept a second, smaller, part of his hoard.

When dealing with humanoids, Valraxaxath often used the polymorph self spell to disguise himself as a wealthy merchant or noble.

Relationships
Valraxaxath engaged in transactional relationships with many chromatic dragons across Faerûn. Otherwise, Valraxaxath was a solitary dragon, but maintained communications with some of the lizardfolk inhabitants of the Great Swamp of Rethild.

## Valargrim Hond

Valargrim Hond a mage of the late 15 century DR and the Magister of 1490 DR to 1496 DR.

Personality
Valargrim Hond was sardonic.

Description
The man was spike-bearded.

Abilities
He was a hardened duelist.

Possessions
Besides a broad selection of rings and wands, Valargrim notably wielded three enchanted flying daggers of his own devising, capable of attacking on their own, and channeling his own spells. The daggers had a sapience of their own.

History
Before becoming the Magister, Valargrim Hond was a mercenary mage who had extensive fighting experience in the Vilhon Reach and Chessenta. In the, after conferring with Mystra, Azuth offered the office of Magister to Valargrim Hond upon the retirement of Tannathra.

He defended his office through numerous duels until the, when he was slain in the Eyes of the Beholder club in Ormpetarr, by six Red Wizards sent by Szass Tam. But his slayer never attained the powers of the office, as they were soon after killed by Handrar Rezgult, a Zhentarim mage who was staying in the city. And before he was able to attain the powers of the office, Valargrim Hond's enchanted daggers struck him down, and then escaped. Thus, Azuth offered the role of Magister to Ramarthant Horaerays.

## Vanra Bergauz

Vanra Bergauz was the only daughter of Lora Bergauz, a retired sailor and a patron of the Blushing Mermaid in the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Vanra looked a lot like her mother. She was a human girl with freckled bronze skin, dark blue eyes, and red hair.

Relationships
Apart from her mother, Vanra had another family member – uncle Tim Twofingers. Even if it was unclear if he was a biological uncle or Lora's friend, Tim adored young Vanra. He gifted her a copy of a book of adventures appropriately called Adventures on the Sea of Fallen Stars.

History
In the, during the time Captain Grisly was replaced by the green hag Auntie Ethel as the owner of the Blushing Mermaid, Vanra was seven years old. During one of her mother's boozy das, Vanra mysteriously disappeared. When Lora came to her senses, Vanra was missing, and everyone at the Mermaid seemed to believe that the girl never existed and Lora was there alone, meeting and drinking with friends. Distraught and desperate, Lora tried to find help, but no one believed her story. She eventually attempted to report the disappearance to Liara Portyr at the Basilisk Gate barracks. The Flaming Fist Blaze's investigation uncovered a lead. Still, when Lora arrived at the barracks, Liara Portyr seemed unable to focus on the problem and talked in circles, not willing nor able to comprehend the crime, as if some mind-magics afflicted her. She may have gotten lucky coming across the group called the Absolute Adversaries, who offered to help the distraught mother.

Vanra was captured by Captain Grisly, or more precisely, Auntie Ethel. The hag consumed the child in a grim act of hag procreation. The heroes could've decided to save the child and, by using the hag's bane potion, extracted the child alive and unharmed from the hag's belly. By other accords, Vanra was rescued, but the adventurers failed to save the child's life, breaking Lora's heart. And the last possibility was the "heroes" taking a grim or cruelly utilitarian approach and allowing the hag to gestate the child into a new hag in exchange for Auntie Ethel's help in defense of the Gate during the War with the Absolute.

## Variel (merchant)

Variel was an employee of Sa'Sani's Mercantile House that operated in the Crossroad Keep in the late  14 century DR.

Description
Variel was a half-elven woman with a light skin tone, blonde hair cut in a bob, and icy blue eyes. She was slender and youthful in appearance. Variel's excitement for her job was barely masked by her professional demeanor. She preferred to wear red and maroon robes.

History
In the, Crossroad Keep was recovered and rebuilt by the allies of Neverwinter. Soon after, two merchants, Il'foss and Kizu, purchased a building in the reopened keep to be used as the headquarters of their trading company. They had big plans to "change the Sword Coast." They hired Variel to serve as the company's majordomo and put her in charge of the home office, as well as allowing her to do trading on the organization's behalf. Soon after she arrived at Crossroad Keep, Variel was tasked with managing the company's few shipments. Il'foss and Kizu grew frustrated with the business being slow; however, when a word game of two massive shipments was sent their way. Variel was never given any details on the nature of the shipments, and she was too happy for the business to look into it. When the shipments reached Crossroad Keep, Il'foss insisted on accompanying one of the shipments to Neverwinter, never to return, and soon after, the second shipment and Kizu disappeared as well. With no leadership, Variel was left alone to keep the headquarters operating.

When merchant Sa'Sani, Il'foss' and Kizu's partner, took over the abandoned headquarters, Variel joined the Mercantile House as the majordomo of Crossroad Keep. She was the point of contact between the forces of Neverwinter, the Greycloaks, as well as the Church of Waukeen, organizing donations, investments, and paying for upgrades to the Keep and its allies on behalf of the Mercantile House. Variel showed initiative in promising the cleric of Waukeen named Daija to build a shrine to her goddess in Crossroad Keep in exchange for the deity's blessing. She also maintained the relationship with the gnomes of Lantan, on Sa'Sani's behalf, and Orias, who operated a portal that connected Crossroad Keep to a secret and illegal logging camp in Samarach via the Lantan-Samarach portal. With time, she hoped to have enough coin to expand the portal's function, connecting the headquarters to other locations across Toril. Another initiative of Variel's was to connect Sa'Sani's Mercantile House with her contact in Neverwinter, which could provide the Greycloaks with improved arms and armor to secure the trade routes around the keep.

## Vampiric dagger

Vampiric daggers were enchanted daggers that drew strength from its victims, created sometime before the late 14 century DR.

Powers
Vampiric daggers had blades of deadly sharpness and tiny serrations. They possessed a powerful +2 enchantment that made the weapon's attacks deadlier and guided the wielder's hand toward their aim. Each successful strike with a vampiric dagger sapped the strength from the target, granting the wielder vampiric regeneration.

History
Sometime after the, a vampiric dagger was handled by the leader of the three remnants of the Shadow Cult that followed the King of Shadows. The remnants of the cult holed up in a ruined fort in the Mere of Dead Men, trying to resurrect the King. The priests used a vampiric dagger against a band of adventurers from Crossroad Keep, sent by Khelgar Ironfist to end the cult once and for all.

## Vale Blossom

The Vale Blossom was a curative flower, a legendary panacea, found growing only in the Dragonbone Vale in the Sword Mountains.

Description
Vale Blossom was a small, vibrant blue flower, often clustered together in patches in the Whetstone Thicket forest of the valley. It was said that the Vale Blossom could cure almost any affliction.

History
The first appearance of the Vale Blossom was recorded by the dwarves of the kingdom of Keldegonn—an ancient fortress in the Dragonbone Vale. That record told the story of a miner who consumed toxic tarnshroom while working in the tunnels underneath the fortress. The miner collapsed with a burning fever and fell into a coma. No medicine nor magic could alleviate that condition. Then his wife met a benevolent dragon, who led her to a patch of flowers in the shade within the Whetstone Thicket. The wife brewed tea using the Vale Blossom and gave it to her husband. Astonished, she noticed that her husband's fever had broke immediately. He was back to full health within a week. People said the miner returned to work in the best shape of his life.

In the late 15 century DR, Elminster Aumar sent a group of adventurers to gather these miraculous flowers, which were a component in a magical ritual that kept the life-draining corruption of the Scaleblight Summit at bay.

## Vargo

Vargo was a skull lord from Acheron who arrived in the Sea of Swords in the late 15 century DR. There, it ruled a cube-shaped island from its Six Face Throne and commanded an army of creatures infected by the rapidly-spreading pixelating curse.

Description
Vargo was an imposing three-headed skeletal figure, and it had modified its body to have space for an additional three heads. One of its heads could only babble, and used a straw to suck from a flask that was tied around Vargo's neck. The flask was filled with water from the Styx, and kept the skull lord free of the pixelating curse, at the cost of affecting the intelligence of its third head.

Personality
Vargo had one personality that was a combination of those of the three creatures that had once been its head. It was a strong leader; cunning and deceptive. It often mixed truth with lies to more easily manipulate those it wanted to. It was egotistical and unemotional. New heads, unless particularly strong-willed, would be completely dominated by Vargo. Each of its heads wrote in a different style of handwriting.

Abilities
By using the Six-Face-Throne, Vargo had the power to control all the creatures affected by the pixelating curse, as well as control all doors and orientation of Cube 1717.

Vargo had discovered a method to attach new heads to its body, which would let it increase in power. While one of its heads was stupefied by the water of the Styx, Vargo was somewhat weakened and lost access to its most powerful spell—finger of death. However, it retained the ability to cast other spells such as eyebite and cloudkill.

Possessions
Vargo owned the Six-Face Throne, a powerful device that it built from the body of the Archquadrone that had originally started the pixelating curse. The Throne had spaces for six heads to control it and Vargo used it to control Cube 1717. However, the Throne could only access the full power of Cube 1717 when it was piloted by a complete set of six heads. Due to one of Vargo's heads being stupefied by the Styx water, Vargo was only able to control two sides of the Throne. Vargo had its second-in-command Sladek operate the third side.

Around its neck, Vargo kept a key to its treasury, and to its chambers.

Vargo was attuned to a set of six ioun stones which it had taken from Sladek. They increased its awareness, wisdom, intelligence, proficiency, defenses, and stored spells for it.

In its chambers, Vargo kept the head of Andromalius, the previous ruler of Cube 1717. The head had become was rotted and pixelated. There was also a sarcophagus made from black steel and lined with red velvet. The chambers were decorated with three sets of items - three tables, three cups. It also owned sixty bottles of expensive wine, a pouch of torpor poison and a spell scroll of dimension door.

Vargo was very wealthy. In its treasury, Vargo stored thousands of coins of various denominations, and many urns of gems. The treasury also contained a  red brazier carved in the shape of a devil's head; and many magic items such as a helm of teleportation, a Horn of Valhalla, and a set of mariner's armor.

Activities
Vargo wished to sever the heads of intelligent individuals and attach them to its own body. It planned to aquire three more skulls, giving it the six necessary to pilot Cube 1717 at full power with the Six-Face Throne. It lured adventurers to the Changing Island in the Sea of Swords, which was truly Cube 1717, hoping to take their heads. Its goal was to conquer all of Faerûn by attacking and pixelating the Sword Coast with a fully powered Cube 1717.

Vargo spent most of its time in its personal chamber of Cube 1717.

Relationships
As the controller of the Six-Faced-Throne, Vargo was in control of all creatures affected by the pixelating curse. Although, they secretly distrusted the skull lord, because it itself was not pixelated like they were. Sladek's forces included pixelated merregons, souls from acheron, and sailors who had discoverd Cube 1717; as well as many pixelated zombies and skeletons.
Vargo always kept its two pixelated bodyguards with it: Astilbe the shadar-kai and a merregon. A notable pixelated creature was Sladek, a genasi who served as second-in-command and controlled one side of the Six-Face Throne.

Vargo was also served by some nonpixelated creatures: Avnas, the derro architect who originally designed Cube 1717; and Simak, a drow who Vargo kept to entertain itself; and Bunch the berbalang. In exchange for bones, Bunch monitored Cube 1717 for any visitors, looking for creatures intelligent enough for Vargo to take their skulls as new heads.
Malika Tahoun was a non-pixelated oinoloth who also served Vargo. She originally came to Toril to destroy Cube 1717. But joined forces with Vargo when it threatened her life. Vargo planned to use her head in the Six-Face Throne, although it hoped to find others.

History

Vargo was originally a trio of three evil adventurers. They joined forces to depose Earl Andromalius, who ruled Cube 1717 in Acheron. But their plot failed, and each of them was charged with sedition, then their heads were cut off and merged together. The three were brought back to life as one being - a skull lord.

The newly-combined Vargo was imprisoned in an oubliette of Cube 1717's prison complex to serve one million life sentences. Hundreds of years later,  as the pixelating curse took hold in Cube 1717, Andromalius was killed and replaced by the Archquadrone. The pixelated soldiers freed the prisoners of Cube 1717, including Vargo. In order to prevent the pixelating curse, the rulers of Acheron struck a bargain with yugoloths to drown Cube 1717 in the Styx. Followers of the Archquadrone desperately sent Cube 1717 to the material plane in an escape. The cube crashed deep into the Sea of Swords on Toril, surrounded by a small sea of potent water from the River Styx.

Vargo found the Archquadrone dying, and made it into the Six-Face Throne. As the master of the Six-Face Throne, Vargo assumed control of Cube 1717. Vargo encountered Avnas the derro, who had been hiding in Cube 1717. It made her swear fealty to it. It tasked her with creating a plumbing system that converted the Styx water that surrounded the cube into mist which kept parts of the cube free of pixelation.

A trireme captained by drow from the underdark came to Cube 1717. The drow had heard of the cube's fall and wanted to use it as a weapon. Vargo attacked the drow, and attemped to take the head of their high priestess, but she killed herself to prevent this. The only survivor was Simak, the captain of the ship. She was secretly protected by her amulet of the styx, but pretended to be stupified by the Styx water.

Cube 1717 was sighted by sailors, who named it the Changing Island. Sladek, a genasi mage, sailed to the island to investigate. He was promptly pixelated by the curse and entered service to Vargo. Not knowing what had happened to Sladek, a diviner named Saberhagen from the Sword Coast, hired adventurers to visit the island and track down the missing genasi. The adventurers sailed to the island, joined by a monodrone. Once they reached Cube 1717, they fought Vargo and the pixelating curse. Vargo was very interested in these adventurers' heads.

## Valana

Personality

Valana was an absent-minded and somewhat amoral arcanist.

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Valana offered her magical expertise to the War Wizards there as the conflict moved to the Demonweb Pits. Taking inspiration from the demonic rituals and blood sacrifices of the drow, she devised a particular enchantment that would allow someone to weaken the Rift Between Worlds using a small sacrifice of vitality, preventing it from destroying the world of Eberron. This mission also pitted the heroes against the draegloth Khaszlokhar, who guarded one of the Orbs of Lolth. ## Vampiric feast

Vampiric feast was a rare epic necromancy spell that allowed the caster to drink in the life force of their opponents, as if a vampire.

Effects
As of the Year of Wild Magic, 1372 DR, casting vampiric feast made the caster drink in the entire life force of every chosen creature within 20 feet (6.1 meters), slaying them instantly and increasing the caster's health. The caster could only absorb enough life force to fully heal themself; the remaining life force dissipated into the fabric of the weave. The life-force of slain creatures also coalesced as a greater shadow, which helped the caster fight. The spell was even able to damage undead creatures.

Accounts varied on if the spell was subject to spell resistance. Fortitude played an important roll in partially resisting the effects of the spell. Individuals with high fortitude were sometimes able to successfully mitigate the effects of the spell, thus losing only about half of their health. This spell was not stopped by death ward or similar effects. Vampiric feast couldn't be countered, interrupted, or dispelled using normal spells or physical attacks. To prevent the spell from being cast, one had to, for example, use an epic version of counterspell.

Development
Before it could be cast, vampiric feast had to be developed. Both divine and arcane casters could develop this spell. Developing an epic spell was different from learning normal spells. Once an epic spell was developed, the caster knew the spell. A developed epic spell became a part of the caster and could even be prepared without a spellbook (if a wizard was the caster). Only those well-versed in spellcraft could develop vampiric feast.

Components
To cast vampiric feast, the caster had to use verbal and somatic components.

## Vaporighu

Vaporighu were a race of foul creatures native to Gehenna and the Dismal Caverns

Description
A vaporighu was a hideous, bloated, waddling blob of slimy hairy flesh, vaguely humanoid in shape. Their skin had the color, and foul smell, of gangrenous rot and dripped with a poisonous slime. Tangled matted fur hung from their bodies, and a pair of small horns protruded from their heads. The skin underneath the fur were a mottled pink/purple mass with visible veins of pulsing bile.their breath came and went in great wheezing gurgles, with and occasional explosion of mucus. They reeked of all the decay and sulphurous stench that encapsulated Gehenna, and would linger for days after the vaporighu had passed through the area.

Personality
All vaporighu were petty, sadistic and voracious, driven by an eternal gnawing hunger they could never satiate. They seemed to perform no other function in the ecosystem of Gehenna, other than to waylay creatures unfortunate enough encounter them, larva and lesser fiend alike.They would pick out hiding places near portals or the rare watering holes and wait for victims to approach, attacking anything less than a greater yugoloth.While they spoke both Abyssal and Infernal, they rarely if ever communicated with others, even their own kind. Whether they disliked each other or just respected their individual hunting grounds no-one can say, but no two vaporighus were ever seen together.

Combat
Vaporighu were tenacious combatants, fighting without any subtlety or art, and to the death. They radiated a powerful aura of fear and horror that could overwhelm even the strongest of opponents, making their foes either flee, or better yet become paralyzed with terror. While they rarely bothered to try to run down fleeing opponents, they delighted in attacking those too terrified to move. They would strike out with their hands, covered in their natural secretions, that would be left behind on a victim. This slime would eat away armor and clothing, and within a few hours turn even the finest armor into pitted rags not worth wearing. They themselves were near immune to damage, only wounded by weapons made from cold iron, even the finest enchanted weapon did no damage unless it was made from that material, and even then the enchantments would do nothing, only the cold iron itself causing any damage.

Rumors & Legends
Some rumors abounded that the vaporighu was once a type of yugoloths that forgot how to leave the plane of Gehenna.

Effects
When the spell was cast, it conjured forth a swarm of vampire bats that were sicked on a target within  of the caster, and the swarm attacked all creatures within the -cube. The swarm health significant damage to all creatures that failed to dodge the critters and pushed them  away from the caster. Some creatures trapped within the spell's area of effect could be swarmed by the summoned vampire bats. ## Valorfoe

This masterfully-crafted weapon was carved with magic runes. A single sentence was written on it in the tongue of the ancient gray dwarves: "Tis Not Death That Is Worthy of Dread, But The Fear of Death."

Valorfoe was created to break the enemy's spirits and resolve in battle in addition to crushing their armor and bodies. By the, Valorfoe ended up in the paws of the leader of ice trolls of Icewind Dale, who allied himself with Iceblood, a mysterious blue-skinned shaman who served godling Arctus and sought to lay waste to the Ten-Towns.

## Vampiric Duality

Vampiric Duality was a book that detailed the nature of vampires. It was written by Telshum Ver Condle and published before in or before the late 15 century DR.

Content
Vampiric Duality was a book plagued by tonal inconsistency. One moment, the author discussed a vampire's two instincts – feeding and procreation, the next, claiming that vampires possessed a personality as faceted as a schizophrenic diamond, then yet again, contradicting itself, talking about them being ideal predators of ravenous hunger with only one thing on ther minds – blood. And yet, the book also claimed that everyone would find vampires' romantic, dark allure, and passion appealing.

History
As of the, a copy of Vampiric Duality book belonged to Araj Oblodra and could be found in her alchemist's lab and potion shop in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate called the Crimson Draughts.

## Valarde's harp

Valarde's harp was a magical instrument capable of stirring the air around itself when played. Though of Myth Drannan origin, it survived the city's fall, and more than six centuries later had become a part of the Harpers' arsenal by .

Description
Valarde's harp, like most instruments of Myth Drannor, was not large, typically between  and  tall. Though it had many strings—twenty, at least, and thirty-six, at most—the harp's modest size meant they were set very close together, which made it even more difficult to play correctly. The highly resonant nature of the brass, copper, and electrum strings meant that half of a skilled harper's effort was dedicated simply to muffling certain chords when they strummed others.

In its original construction, Valarde's harp was nearly triangular. The wood pieces that comprised the instrument were polished to the point of shining, regardless of the material's dark coloration—a deep red and black that, itself, was elegantly tessellated by ivory and gilt. Three such pieces made up the archetypal Myth Drannan harp: a fore-pillar, a neck, and a body. The first, which curled outward, was the segment furthest from the player, forming the harp's front edge; the second formed the top of the harp, bearing the pins around which the instrument's strings were tuned; and the third was held to the harper's shoulder, supporting the soundboard that, itself, held to the bottom of the strings.

Powers
The magic within a Valarde's harp, as with any of its Myth Drannan peers, emanated an aura of good, which distinguished it from an ordinary instrument. What further set Valarde's harp apart was its control over currents of air, an effect shaped into two possible forms at the whims of the harp's player: a wall or a gust. Either one emerged powerfully from the instrument's body after a minute of consistent strumming, but the former persisted an added three minutes if not willed to end early.

There was also a secondary property to the magic in a Valarde's harp, one minor in comparison to the stirring of winds. Whenever the instrument was played, the magic inside coalesced into small beads of glowing light, which were capable of weaving and winking around the wielder's head, as well as the harp's own strings.

It was unfortunate, however, that it was possible for Valarde's harp to lose its enchantment. Though its metallic strings could be replaced at leisure, for they were not the source of its powers, it would be impossible to regain the original magic if ever the harp's fine, wooden form was broken. Even mending the pieces had no restorative effects on the harp's unique abilities.

History
The utopic Myth Drannor was once home to many master craftsmen of human, elven, or mixed lineage, and each enchanted countless items, of which Valarde's harp was a prime example. Even so, the fall of the city was calamitous; despite the prevalence of such beautiful instruments, few were the Myth Drannan harps that survived, and fewer yet retained the Art that had once been imbued into them.

By the mid–14 century DR, hundreds of years later, only nine types were known to Elminster, the renowned sage. Among them was Valarde's harp and, according to the Old Mage's estimates, there may have been at least twenty such harps, with their dweomers unspoiled, still on Faerûn.

Reputation
The beauty and unique abilities of Valarde's harp were only two of the many reasons that the instrument was sought after. As with its fellows, what truly elevated its renown was that the abilities of this Myth Drannan harp were able to be activated by anybody, requiring no specific training or attunement—they were not tied to a single kind of humanoid or alignment, nor to the musical talents of a bard. This simple fact, then, meant diverse groups and people prized Valarde's harp highly, including even figures as storied as Elminster.

Nevertheless, this did not mean that Valarde's harps without their enchantment were considered worthless. By the value of their craftsmanship alone, many earned a place in the personal collections of various monarchs and mages.

Notable Owners
 The Harpers were known to equip themselves, fittingly, with enchanted harps of many sorts, Valarde's harp included.

Appendix
See Also
 Scrolls of the Harpers were used to temporarily transform Harper pins into Valarde's harps and other such Myth Drannan instruments.

## Valeanne

Valeanne was a female eladrin residing in Karador, the capital of Sarifal, on the island of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles as of the mid-to-late 15 century DR. She was a key participant in a plot to save the leShay princess Amaranth from the wrath of the girl's half-sister, High Lady Ordalf, and died while helping the princess escape from Sarifal.

Personality

She gave her life to keep her promises and to save the life of the young Amaranth, although she was not be happy about it. She believed that doing the right thing was not the same as doing a good thing. She could be manipulative and merciless.

Relationships
Valeanne was of high enough station to be referred to as "madam" within Sarifal's court. She had a caretaking role over the young Amaranth, who referred to her as mistress.

History

Valeanne made a promise to safeguard the youngest daughter of Princess Callia of the leShay, Amaranth, from the wrath and jealousy of her elder daughter, Ordalf. Soon after, Callia and Amaranth's father were both killed by Prince Araithe, Ordalf's son.

Soon after, in the late 1460s DR, Ordalf gave birth to a sickly son that lived only a few days. Realizing that it was time to remove Amaranth from Ordalf's grasp for her protection. Valeanne conspired with Tiana and Shamasar to kidnap Princess Amaranth from Karador and transport her to the court of Queen Erliza Daressin on Snowdown. The plan was to awaken the nine-year-old before dawn on the day that the dying baby was to be anointed, flee the city before first light, get fresh horses at Glenraugh, and then ride up to Crane Point, where a pair of hippogriff riders would pick them up and fly them to Snowdown. They told the girl (who remained oblivious to the threat to her life) that they were planning to meet her sister on the shores of lake Ulls, and would enjoy some mulled wine as they watched the griffons at Corwell's Steeple. Valeanne and Shamasar, accompanied by five of Shamasar's dragonborn soldiers, departed from Karador while Tiana had already gone ahead to Snowdown.

Unfortunately, their plan was betrayed, and a host of drow, cyclopes, and driders ambushed them as they neared Crane Point, killing Shamasar and his troops, incinerating one of the hippogriffs, and badly wounding the remaining rider. Tired from the journey, the petulant Amaranth proved uncooperative as Valeanne tried to navigate the battlefield, forcing her to lie to and manhandle the girl as well as to execute her beloved pony, Flower, right in front of her. She managed to hand off the girl to the surviving hippogriff, who flew away as she tried to say goodbye. She then waited to be set upon by the drow,

The hippogriff rider would subsequently succumb to his wounds after veering off in the wrong direction, dropping Amaranth onto Moray and causing her to be lost to the leShay for a decade. Meanwhile, Valeanne was remembered in death as a traitor to Sarifal.

## Vanda Volahrn

Vanda Volahrn was a wizard, owner of the Pearled Harp trading coster, and one of the few female pashas in the city of Calimport, Calimshan, in the late 14 century DR.

Relationships
She was the half-Calishite daughter of a courtesan in Suzail, Cormyr, and of Pasha Samiir el Volahrn. She was his sole surviving offspring.This implies she had half-siblings who'd died, apparently murdered, given how her father's rivals later target her, but another possibility is the deadly Trades Conflagration of 1369 DR, which could've caused the whole family to perish.

History
Vanda was living with her mother in Suzail and unaware of her birthright when, in the late 1360s DR,This is "recently" as of Flamerule in 1370 DR. agents of Syl-Pasha Ralan el Pesarkhal informed her of her father's passing. Furthermore, they offered her Samiir's status as pasha and ownership of his trading coster, the Pearled Harp, provided she used it to support certain trade alliances beneficial to Calimshan's ruler.

Vanda accepted this and moved to Calimport, becoming suddenly wealthy and one of the only female pashas there. Her takeover of the Pearled Harp blocked rival merchants who coveted it for themselves.

Activities
Now much wealthier and resourced, Vanda was satisfied with focusing on her studies of magic whilst leaving the everyday running of the Pearled Harp, also called Purple Pearls, trading coster to its employees in Calimport and Calimshan. She stayed alert to local politics, despite what others thought, and was well aware of the jealous rival merchants who desired her death as well so they might take control of the business.

Home
Vanda lived in the Pearls, a fine house in Azhaya Drudach, Marekh Sabban, in the city's Emerald Ward. Her house reached three stories and had an outer walled yard.

## Varney

Varney was the innkeeper at the Dragon Defeated tavern of Halfknot in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
Varney was a clever entrepreneur, who happened to chronically blame everyone else for his mishaps.

Activities
He owned and worked at the Dragon Defeated tavern.

Relationships
He was married.

History
The owner and innkeeper at the Dragon Defeated tavern, he hosted the painter Petra in early ; she offered to pay for her stay by painting a new sign for his tavern. He agreed, and asked that his sign feature three tavern regulars, Wyrmbait Nix, Silvenestri Silver, and Badger Bates, as dragonslayers over a green dragon. Then he established a weekly contest for guests at the inn and tavern, where they'd tell tales of dragons, and the most impressive tale every evening would have a coin from each listener. His three regulars would be the judges.

By the end of winter, as Petra prepared to leave, she called out his three judges and showed she was an expert in painting dragons; she showed all the artworks she was carrying, and told of her extensive efforts to draw dragons correctly. Despite her showing more of dragons than anyone had all season, Badger wasn't willing to grant her the prize unless she proved she'd too seen Malaeragoth; Varney gave her permission to draw him on the wall. She stopped at the eyes, which Badger claimed as proof she hadn't seen them, still withholding the prize. Varney agreed with Badger, and offended, she drew the eyes, took the prize, and left without a word. Immediately, the picture turned into Malaeragoth, which leapt out and ate Badger, damaging much of the tavern. In the ensuing chaos, the sign was damaged, leaving only the princess in one piece.

Varney later rebranded the tavern as the Royal Rescue, which led to an unexplained incident in time.

## Va'aunsh Avithoul

Va'aunsh Avithoul was a member of the drow House Avithoul of Sschindylryn in ancient days.

History
Va'aunsh was a son of the Matron Va'arrej Avithoul, and he won a measure of fame and respect by inventing the first of the drow slave collars after the Great Revolt. House Avithoul went on to use derivations of this collar to control and profit off slaves, earning them a place of prominence in the city of Sschindylryn.

The collar that he created was placed on display in the House Avithoul estate, proudly displayed alongside Knee-bender and the skull of his mother Va'arrej. When Cormyrean forces counterattacked during the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, they raided the estate and freed their slaves. They may have also destroyed the relic as final jab.

## Va'arrej Avithoul

Matron Va'arrej Avithoul was the founder of the drow House Avithoul of Sschindylryn in ancient days.

History
Va'arrej was a pioneer among the early drow who inhabited Sschindylryn, and was exceptionally warlike. She led raids through the city's portals to distant places while wielding Knee-bender, and started the tradition of taking slaves to enrich herself. She used this wealth and influence to create the noble house ruled by her daughters. Following the Great Revolt, her son Va'aunsh Avithoul invented the first drow slave collar and cemented their place as masters of slavery.

Following her death her skull was preserved and put on display in the estate of House Avithoul, proudly placed alongside Knee-bender and the first drow slave collar. In the time of her descendent Balbaste Avithoul, her house took part in the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar. When Cormyrean forces counterattacked at the behest of Mirini Elemys they raided the estate and freed Avithoul's slaves, and may have also destroyed Va'arrej's skull as an opportunistic insult.

## Valindra's crown

Valindra's crown was a legendary enchanted crown once worn by Valindra Shadowmantle, the lich elf, who plagued Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Valindra's crown, and, possibly, its copies, was a sinister-looking headpiece decorated with five sharp spikes reaching up to the skies. The crown was made of dark metal and lacked any ornamentations typical for that type of jewelry.

Powers
When worn, or even simply touched, it echoed whisperings of an unknown entity directly into the mind. The crown's true enchantments available to Valindra herself were unknown; however, when the crown was lost after the siege of Neverwinter, its magic allowed the wearers to boost their physical and magical might, their ability to deal devastating, well-aimed, or lucky damage, sped up their recovery time, and slightly increased their physical defenses.

History
In the late 15 century DR, the, the city of Neverwinter was besieged by the armies led by Valindra Shadowmantle. After her defeat at the hands of the Heroes of Sleeping Dragon Bridge, her crown was lost, and, eventually, recovered by the city's defenders and adventurers.
